<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every nonempty finite oriented graph has a vertex with at least as many vertices at directed distance two as at directed distance one. This resolves Seymour's second neighborhood conjecture positively.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Every nonempty finite oriented graph has a vertex $v$ such that $$\lvert N_1^+(v)\rvert\leq \lvert N_2^+(v)\rvert.$$
  - Explanation: Resolves Seymour's second neighborhood conjecture positively for every nonempty finite oriented graph without connectivity or degree restrictions, establishing that some vertex has at least as many vertices at directed distance two as at directed distance one.
- **C2** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-counterexample-reduction)): If Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> has a counterexample, then there is a finite nonempty oriented graph $G$ in which every vertex has positive indegree and $$\label{eq:strict-subset-growth} \lvert U\rvert+\lvert F_G^2(U)\rvert<2\lvert F_G(U)\rvert \qquad\text{for every }\varnothing\ne U\subsetneq V(G).$$
  - Explanation: Shows that any hypothetical counterexample to Theorem 1 implies the existence of a nonempty finite oriented graph where every vertex has positive indegree and every nonempty proper vertex subset has a first image strictly larger than the average of the subset and its second image.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-subset-deficit)): Let $D$ be a finite nonempty oriented graph such that $\lvert N_1^+(v)\rvert>\lvert N_2^+(v)\rvert$ for every $v\in V(D)$. Choose $D$ with minimum order and, subject to that, minimum number of arcs among all graphs with this property. Then $D$ is strongly connected, every vertex has positive indegree, and $$\label{eq:subset-deficit} \lvert F^2(S)\setminus F(S)\rvert<\lvert F(S)\setminus S\rvert \qquad\text{for every }\varnothing\ne S\subsetneq V(D).$$
  - Explanation: Establishes that a minimal counterexample ordered first by vertex count and then by arc count is strongly connected, has positive indegrees at all vertices, and satisfies a strict boundary expansion deficit for every nonempty proper vertex subset.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-blowup)): Let $D$ be an oriented graph on $n\ge1$ vertices, all of positive indegree, satisfying <a href="#eq:subset-deficit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:subset-deficit">[eq:subset-deficit]</a> for every nonempty proper subset of $V(D)$. For an integer $m>n$, replace each vertex by a transitive tournament on $m$ vertices. For every arc $v\to w$ of $D$, put all arcs from the block at $v$ to the block at $w$, and put no other arcs between distinct blocks. The resulting graph $G$ is oriented, all its indegrees are positive, and it satisfies <a href="#eq:strict-subset-growth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:strict-subset-growth">[eq:strict-subset-growth]</a>.
  - Explanation: Constructs the lexicographic product of a base oriented graph satisfying the subset deficit with a transitive tournament of order strictly exceeding the base order, amplifying the integer subset deficit into the strict subset growth inequality.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pruning)): Let $X$ be a finite set with a binary relation denoted by $\to$, and let $\mathcal R,\mathcal C\subseteq X\times X$ be two families, regarded as separate copies even when their coordinates agree. Say that $(p,j)\in\mathcal R$ and $(i,s)\in\mathcal C$ *conflict* if $$p\to i\qquad\text{and}\qquad s\to j.$$ Define $$\begin{aligned} Z&=\{(i,j):\text{some }(p,j)\in\mathcal R \text{ conflicts with some }(i,s)\in\mathcal C\},\\ H&=\{(p,s):\text{some }(p,j)\in\mathcal R \text{ conflicts with some }(i,s)\in\mathcal C\}. \end{aligned}$$ A point $(i,j)$ is *covered* by a member $(p,j)\in\mathcal R$ when $p\to i$, and by a member $(i,s)\in\mathcal C$ when $s\to j$. These are the only ways a member covers a point.* *There are subfamilies $\mathcal R'\subseteq\mathcal R$ and $\mathcal C'\subseteq\mathcal C$ with no conflicts between them such that every initially conflict-free member is retained and $$\label{eq:pruning-bound} \lvert \mathcal R\setminus\mathcal R'\rvert+ \lvert \mathcal C\setminus\mathcal C'\rvert \leq \lvert H\rvert+ \lvert \{z\in Z:z\text{ is covered by no member of } \mathcal R'\cup\mathcal C'\}\rvert.$$ The two sides are counted separately throughout.
  - Explanation: Establishes for any finite binary relation that two families of pairs can be pruned to eliminate all conflicts while retaining every initially conflict-free member, with total deletions bounded by the size of H plus the number of points in Z left uncovered after pruning.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-kernel-support)): Let $T\subseteq I\times J$, where $I$ and $J$ are finite. Suppose that $D:\mathbb R^J\to\mathbb R^I$ has maximum rank among all real matrices supported on $T$. Then, for every $x\in\ker D$, every $y\in\ker D^{\mathsf T}$, and every $(s,j)\in T$, one has $x_jy_s=0$.
  - Explanation: Proves that if a real matrix achieves maximum rank among all real matrices supported on a finite relation T, then any kernel vector of the matrix and any kernel vector of its transpose have vanishing coordinate product at every supported pair in T.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-no-concavity)): There is no nonempty finite oriented graph $G$ in which every vertex has an in-neighbor and whose one-step image operator $$F(U)=\{y\in V(G):x\to y\text{ for some }x\in U\}$$ satisfies $$\label{eq:impossible-concavity} \lvert U\rvert+\lvert F^2(U)\rvert<2\lvert F(U)\rvert \qquad(\varnothing\ne U\subsetneq V(G)),$$ where $F^2=F\circ F$.
  - Explanation: Rules out the existence of any nonempty finite oriented graph with all positive indegrees whose one-step image operator satisfies the strict subset growth inequality on all nonempty proper vertex subsets.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-weighted-forms)): Let $D$ be a nonempty finite oriented graph, and write $A(D)$ for its arc set. For a vertex weighting $\eta:V(D)\to[0,\infty)$, write $\eta(S)=\sum_{x\in S}\eta(x)$. Then the following hold.* 1. *For every vertex weighting $\eta:V(D)\to[0,\infty)$, there is a vertex $v\in V(D)$ such that $$\eta(N_1^+(v))\leq\eta(N_2^+(v)).$$* 2. *There is a vertex weighting $\eta:V(D)\to[0,\infty)$ with $\sum_{x\in V(D)}\eta(x)=1$ such that $$\eta(N_1^+(v))\leq\eta(N_2^+(v))\qquad\text{for every }v\in V(D).$$ The entries of this weighting need not all be positive.* 3. *For every arc weighting $w:A(D)\to[0,\infty)$, extend the notation by setting $w(vs)=0$ when $vs\notin A(D)$. For $v,s\in V(D)$, put $$\beta_v^w(s)=\max\Bigl(\{0\}\cup \{w(us)-w(vs):u\in V(D),\ v\to u\to s\}\Bigr).$$ Then there is a vertex $v\in V(D)$ such that $$\sum_{s\in V(D)}\beta_v^w(s) \geq \sum_{u\in N_1^+(v)}w(vu).$$ The maximum is zero if there is no indicated two-step path. Such paths are determined by the arcs of $D$, so an arc of weight zero is still present and is not treated as a missing arc.
  - Explanation: Establishes the vertex-weighted and arc-weighted versions of the second neighborhood conjecture on any nonempty finite oriented graph, including the existence of a suitable vertex for any nonnegative vertex weighting, the existence of a normalized vertex weighting suitable for all vertices simultaneously, and the arc-weighted inequality.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-directed-cycle-consequences)): Let $D$ be a nonempty finite oriented graph of order $n$. For $v\in V(D)$, write $$N_1^-(v)=\{u:u\to v\},\qquad d^\pm(v)=\lvert N_1^\pm(v)\rvert,\qquad \delta^\pm(D)=\min_{x\in V(D)}d^\pm(x).$$ Define the non-neighbors of $v$, excluding $v$ itself, by $$Z(v)=V(D)\setminus \bigl(\{v\}\cup N_1^+(v)\cup N_1^-(v)\bigr).$$ Then the following hold.* 1. *If $D$ has no directed $3$-cycle, then some vertex $v$ satisfies $$d^+(v)\leq\lvert Z(v)\rvert,\qquad n-1\geq 2d^+(v)+d^-(v).$$* 2. *If $\delta^+(D)\geq n/3$ and $\delta^-(D)\geq n/3$, then $D$ contains a directed $3$-cycle.* 3. *If $r\geq1$ is an integer, $d^+(v)=d^-(v)=r$ for every vertex, and $D$ has directed girth exactly four, then $n\geq3r+1$.* *Here directed girth is the length of a shortest directed cycle.
  - Explanation: Deduces structural degree and order bounds for oriented graphs from Theorem 1: in triangle-free oriented graphs some vertex has outdegree at most its non-neighbor count; minimum indegree and outdegree at least n/3 force a directed 3-cycle; and r-regular oriented graphs of directed girth 4 have order at least 3r+1.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **oriented graph** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An *oriented graph* is a finite directed graph with no loops, no multiple arcs, and no pair of opposite arcs.
- **first and exact second out-neighborhoods** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a vertex $v$, write $$N_1^+(v)=\{u:v\to u\},\qquad N_2^+(v)=\{w\notin\{v\}\cup N_1^+(v): \text{ there is a vertex }u\text{ with }v\to u\to w\}.$$
- **tournament** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A *tournament* is an oriented graph with an arc between every pair of distinct vertices.
- **one-step and two-step set-image operators** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For a digraph $D$ and a set $S$ of its vertices, define $$F_D(S)=\{y:\text{ there is }x\in S\text{ with }x\to y\}, \qquad F_D^2(S)=F_D(F_D(S)).$$
- **conflict between left and right pairs** ([source](#stmt-lem-pruning)): Say that $(p,j)\in\mathcal R$ and $(i,s)\in\mathcal C$ *conflict* if $$p\to i\qquad\text{and}\qquad s\to j.$$
- **conflict index sets Z and H** ([source](#stmt-lem-pruning)): Define $$\begin{aligned} Z&=\{(i,j):\text{some }(p,j)\in\mathcal R \text{ conflicts with some }(i,s)\in\mathcal C\},\\ H&=\{(p,s):\text{some }(p,j)\in\mathcal R \text{ conflicts with some }(i,s)\in\mathcal C\}. \end{aligned}$$
- **covered point** ([source](#stmt-lem-pruning)): A point $(i,j)$ is *covered* by a member $(p,j)\in\mathcal R$ when $p\to i$, and by a member $(i,s)\in\mathcal C$ when $s\to j$.
- **matrix supported on T** ([source](#sec:pruning)): For finite index sets $I,J$, a matrix supported on $T\subseteq I\times J$ means a real matrix whose entries outside $T$ are zero; entries in $T$ are allowed to vanish.
- **coordinate image operators F_1 and F_2** ([source](#sec:incompatibility)): For $S\subseteq X\times X$, define the two coordinate image operators by $$\begin{aligned} F_1S&=\{(i,j): (p,j)\in S\text{ and }p\to i \text{ for some }p\in X\},\\ F_2S&=\{(i,j): (i,s)\in S\text{ and }s\to j \text{ for some }s\in X\}. \end{aligned}$$
- **compatible pair families** ([source](#sec:incompatibility)): Call $P,Q\subseteq X\times X$ *compatible* if $F_1P\cap F_2Q=\varnothing$.
- **extremal objective quantities M and d** ([source](#sec:incompatibility)): Thus we may choose, among all compatible pairs with $\Delta\subseteq P\cap Q$, one maximizing $M+d$, where $$M=\lvert P\rvert+\lvert Q\rvert,\qquad d=\lvert X\rvert^2-\lvert F_1P\rvert-\lvert F_2Q\rvert.$$
- **arc-weight gain function beta_v^w(s)** ([source](#stmt-cor-weighted-forms)): For $v,s\in V(D)$, put $$\beta_v^w(s)=\max\Bigl(\{0\}\cup \{w(us)-w(vs):u\in V(D),\ v\to u\to s\}\Bigr).$$
- **in-neighborhood, degrees, and minimum degrees** ([source](#stmt-cor-directed-cycle-consequences)): For $v\in V(D)$, write $$N_1^-(v)=\{u:u\to v\},\qquad d^\pm(v)=\lvert N_1^\pm(v)\rvert,\qquad \delta^\pm(D)=\min_{x\in V(D)}d^\pm(x).$$
- **non-neighbor set Z(v)** ([source](#stmt-cor-directed-cycle-consequences)): Define the non-neighbors of $v$, excluding $v$ itself, by $$Z(v)=V(D)\setminus \bigl(\{v\}\cup N_1^+(v)\cup N_1^-(v)\bigr).$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | \lvert N_1^+(v)\rvert | ≤ | $\lvert N_2^+(v)\rvert$ | Unconditional over all nonempty finite oriented graphs; holds for at least one vertex v |
| Q2 | \lvert F^2(S)\setminus F(S)\rvert | < | $\lvert F(S)\setminus S\rvert$ | Every nonempty proper vertex subset S of V(D) |
| Q3 | m | > | $n$ | Integer block size m strictly greater than base graph order n >= 1 |
| Q4 | \lvert T\rvert-2\lvert f(T)\rvert+\lvert f^2(T)\rvert | ≤ | $1$ | Any nonempty subset T of {1, ..., m} |
| Q5 | \lvert \mathcal R\setminus\mathcal R'\rvert+ \lvert \mathcal C\setminus\mathcal C'\rvert | ≤ | $\lvert H\rvert+ \lvert \{z\in Z:z\text{ is covered by no member of } \mathcal R'\cup\mathcal C'\}\rvert$ | All finite sets X and pair families R, C |
| Q6 | \lvert \mathcal M\rvert | ≤ | $\lvert Z\rvert+\lvert H\rvert$ | Maximum matching M of size k + ell + t in Gamma |
| Q7 | \lvert H\rvert | ≤ | $d$ | Conflict source set H of complement families R and C bounded by uncovered point count d |
| Q8 | \sum_{x\in V(D)}\eta(x) | = | $1$ | Normalized vertex weighting eta satisfying the second-neighborhood inequality at every vertex |
| Q9 | n-1 | ≥ | $2d^+(v)+d^-(v)$ | All n >= 1; holds for at least one vertex v in V(D) |
| Q10 | \delta^+(D), \delta^-(D) | ≥ | $n/3$ | Minimum outdegree and minimum indegree threshold forcing a directed 3-cycle |
| Q11 | n | ≥ | $3r+1$ | Every integer r >= 1 |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **S1** [claim](#stmt-lem-subset-deficit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-subset-deficit): Minimal counterexample selection (minimum order, then minimum arc count) combined with iterative arc deletion from a nonempty proper subset S to its exterior expansion E \ T. -> Strong connectivity of D, positive indegree at every vertex, and strict subset deficit \|F^2(S) \ F(S)\| < \|F(S) \ S\|.
- **S2** [claim](#stmt-lem-blowup) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-blowup): Lexicographic product blowup replacing each vertex of D by a transitive tournament on m > n vertices and placing all arcs between blocks along arcs of D. -> Oriented graph G with positive indegrees satisfying \|U\| + \|F_G^2(U)\| < 2\|F_G(U)\| for every nonempty proper subset U of V(G).
- **S3** [claim](#stmt-prop-counterexample-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-blowup-2): Composition of the minimal counterexample subset deficit (Lemma 3) and the transitive-tournament blowup (Lemma 4) for m > \|V(D)\|. -> Existence of a nonempty finite oriented graph G with positive indegrees satisfying the strict subset growth inequality on all nonempty proper subsets if Theorem 1 has a counterexample.
- **S4** [claim](#stmt-lem-kernel-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel-support): Rank-one single-entry perturbation D_tau = D + tau e_s e_j^T on the support T to contradict maximum support rank if x_j y_s != 0. -> Orthogonality condition x_j y_s = 0 for every (s, j) in T.
- **S5** [claim](#stmt-lem-pruning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel-support-2): Construction of an augmented bipartite graph Gamma, polynomial nonvanishing for simultaneous generic matrix ranks of four commuting operators NL = AB and support matrices D^{p,i}, kernel lifting via Lemma 6 to bound maximum matching size by \|Z\| + \|H\|, and alternating-path vertex cover construction. -> Conflict-free subfamilies R' and C' retaining all initially conflict-free members with total deletions bounded by \|H\| plus uncovered points of Z.
- **S6** [claim](#stmt-prop-no-concavity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-no-concavity): Extremal maximization of M + d over compatible pair families containing the diagonal, fiberwise summation of strict subset growth on columns and rows, complementation to obtain \|R\| + \|C\| > M + 2d, and conflict pruning via Lemma 5 to strictly increase M + d. -> Contradiction ruling out the existence of any oriented graph G satisfying Proposition 2.
- **S7** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-no-concavity-2): Proof by contradiction combining the counterexample reduction (Proposition 2) with the impossibility of the strict subset growth graph (Proposition 7), followed by deduction of weighted and cycle corollaries (Corollaries 8 and 9). -> Theorem 1 (every nonempty finite oriented graph has a vertex v with \|N_1^+(v)\| <= \|N_2^+(v)\|) and Corollaries 8 and 9.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I1**: Every nonempty finite oriented graph has a vertex $v$ satisfying $\lvert N_1^+(v)\rvert\leq\lvert N_2^+(v)\rvert$, where $N_2^+(v)=F_D^2(\{v\})\setminus F_D(\{v\})$ counts distinct vertices at directed distance two.
- **I2**: In a minimal counterexample $D$ satisfying $\lvert N_1^+(v)\rvert>\lvert N_2^+(v)\rvert$ for every $v\in V(D)$, chosen first with minimum order and then with minimum number of arcs, $D$ is strongly connected, every vertex has positive indegree, and every nonempty proper subset $S\subsetneq V(D)$ has two-step expansion strictly smaller than $\lvert F(S)\setminus S\rvert$.
- **I3**: Within a transitive tournament $T_m$ on $m$ vertices labeled $1,\ldots,m$ with arcs from smaller to larger labels, every nonempty label set $T$ with least element $a$ satisfies $f(T)=\{a+1,\ldots,m\}$ and has internal second-difference bounded above by $1$; replacing each vertex of an oriented graph $D$ on $n\ge1$ vertices with positive indegrees satisfying the subset deficit by $T_m$ with $m>n$ produces an oriented graph $G$ via the lexicographic product $D[T_m]$ with positive indegrees where the one-step image $F_G(U)$ strictly exceeds the average of $U$ and its second image for every $\varnothing\ne U\subsetneq V(G)$.
- **I4**: For any finite set $X$ with a binary relation $\to$ and two pair families $\mathcal R,\mathcal C\subseteq X\times X$ counted as separate copies, a maximum matching $\mathcal M$ in the augmented bipartite graph $\Gamma$ satisfies $\lvert \mathcal M\rvert=k+\ell+t\leq\lvert Z\rvert+\lvert H\rvert$, and the corresponding vertex cover $K$ of size at most $\lvert Z\rvert+\lvert H\rvert$ yields conflict-free subfamilies $\mathcal R'\subseteq\mathcal R$ and $\mathcal C'\subseteq\mathcal C$ retaining all initially conflict-free members with total deletions at most the size of $H$ plus $q$, where $q$ is the number of points in $Z$ left uncovered.
- **I5**: In every nonempty finite oriented graph $D$, for every vertex weighting $\eta:V(D)\to[0,\infty)$ there is a vertex $v\in V(D)$ where $\eta(N_1^+(v))$ is at most $\eta(N_2^+(v))$; simultaneously there exists a normalized vertex weighting $\eta:V(D)\to[0,\infty)$ with $\sum_{x\in V(D)}\eta(x)=1$ where $\eta(N_1^+(v))$ is at most $\eta(N_2^+(v))$ for every $v\in V(D)$; and for every arc weighting $w:A(D)\to[0,\infty)$ there is a vertex $v\in V(D)$ whose total two-step gain over endpoints $s$ in $V(D)$ is at least its outgoing arc weight sum.
- **I6**: In any nonempty finite oriented graph $D$ of order $n$: if $D$ has no directed $3$-cycle, then $N_2^+(v)\subseteq Z(v)$ for every $v$ and some vertex $v$ has outdegree at most its non-neighbor count; if $\delta^+(D)\geq n/3$ and $\delta^-(D)\geq n/3$, then $D$ contains a directed $3$-cycle; and if $r\geq1$ is an integer with $d^+(v)=d^-(v)=r$ for every vertex and directed girth four, then $n\geq3r+1$.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **AU1**: Applying the second-neighborhood inequality $\lvert N_1^+(v)\rvert\leq\lvert N_2^+(v)\rvert$ directly to arbitrary directed graphs such as general call graphs, state machines, or multigraphs. -> A general directed graph with opposite arcs, self-loops, or parallel arcs can violate the inequality at every vertex because two-step walks return to $v$ or stay inside the first out-neighborhood.
- **AU2**: Computing the second out-neighborhood size by taking the full two-step set image $F^2(S)$ or by counting the number of length-two directed walks $v\to u\to w$. -> Including vertices already reached in one step or counting multiple two-step paths to the same endpoint inflates the second-neighborhood count and invalidates the comparison with the first out-neighborhood.
- **AU3**: Pruning zero-weight arcs from the graph before evaluating two-step paths in the arc-weighted inequality of Corollary 8. -> Dropping zero-weight arcs where $w(vs)=0$ removes valid two-step paths whose difference $w(us)-w(vs)$ is positive, artificially lowering the two-step maximum and falsifying the arc-weighted guarantee.
- **AU4**: Bounding the number of deleted members in Lemma 5 by the size of $H$ alone, or assuming that the non-neighbor inclusion $N_2^+(v)\subseteq Z(v)$ of Corollary 9 holds in oriented graphs containing directed triangles. -> Omitting the uncovered-points count $q$ in Lemma 5 underestimates the required vertex cover size $\lvert Z\rvert+\lvert H\rvert$, and directed $3$-cycles allow a second out-neighbor $w\in N_2^+(v)$ to satisfy $w\in N_1^-(v)$.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The main theorem, transitive-tournament block bound, bipartite-matching conflict pruning, vertex- and arc-weighted equivalences, and triangle-free non-neighbor bounds operate on finite oriented graphs and finite binary relations and admit exact finite algorithms.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with vertex count n, oriented arcs, vertex_weights, arc_weights, blowup_m, and pruning families R and C
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= n <= 7; arcs form a finite oriented graph with no loops, duplicate arcs, or opposite arcs
# OUTPUTS: JSON object with seymour_vertices, max_slack, min_subset_deficit, universal_eta, weighted_vertices, arc_weighted_vertices, blowup_max_lhs, pruning result, and c3_free/non_neighbor_vertices
# TIME: O(2^n * n + n^3 + |V_L| * |E_Gamma|) for subset expansion, neighborhoods, exact rational simplex, and Konig bipartite pruning
# SPACE: O(n^2 + |R| + |C|)
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic via python-flint (flint.fmpq) and exact integer bitmasks
# FAILURE: Exits nonzero on invalid JSON, violated oriented-graph preconditions, or unsolvable LP
import sys, json
from collections import deque
import flint

Q = flint.fmpq

def solve_universal_eta(n, out1, out2):
    m = n + 1
    total_vars = 2 * n + 1
    T = [[Q(0) for _ in range(total_vars + 1)] for _ in range(m + 1)]
    basis = [n + i for i in range(n)] + [2 * n]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if (out2[i] >> j) & 1:
                T[i][j] = Q(-1)
            elif (out1[i] >> j) & 1:
                T[i][j] = Q(1)
        T[i][n + i] = Q(1)
    for j in range(n):
        T[n][j] = Q(1)
        T[m][j] = Q(-1)
    T[n][2 * n] = Q(1)
    T[n][total_vars] = Q(1)
    T[m][total_vars] = Q(-1)
    while True:
        col = -1
        for c in range(2 * n):
            if T[m][c] < 0:
                col = c
                break
        if col == -1:
            break
        best_r = -1
        best_ratio = None
        for r in range(m):
            if T[r][col] > 0:
                ratio = T[r][total_vars] / T[r][col]
                if best_r == -1 or ratio < best_ratio or (ratio == best_ratio and basis[r] < basis[best_r]):
                    best_r = r
                    best_ratio = ratio
        if best_r == -1:
            sys.exit(2)
        piv = T[best_r][col]
        for c in range(total_vars + 1):
            T[best_r][c] /= piv
        for r in range(m + 1):
            if r != best_r and T[r][col] != 0:
                factor = T[r][col]
                for c in range(total_vars + 1):
                    T[r][c] -= factor * T[best_r][c]
        basis[best_r] = col
    eta = [Q(0) for _ in range(n)]
    for r in range(m):
        if basis[r] < n:
            eta[basis[r]] = T[r][total_vars]
    return [str(x) for x in eta]

def prune_families(out1, R_in, C_in):
    R = [tuple(x) for x in R_in]
    C = [tuple(x) for x in C_in]
    Z_set, H_set = set(), set()
    conflicts = []
    for r_idx, (p, j) in enumerate(R):
        for c_idx, (i, s) in enumerate(C):
            if ((out1[p] >> i) & 1) and ((out1[s] >> j) & 1):
                Z_set.add((i, j))
                H_set.add((p, s))
                conflicts.append((r_idx, c_idx))
    Z = sorted(Z_set)
    H = sorted(H_set)
    V_L = [("R", k) for k in range(len(R))] + [("ZL", k) for k in range(len(Z))]
    V_R = [("C", k) for k in range(len(C))] + [("ZR", k) for k in range(len(Z))]
    adj = {u: [] for u in V_L}
    for r_idx, (p, j) in enumerate(R):
        for z_idx, (zi, zj) in enumerate(Z):
            if j == zj and ((out1[p] >> zi) & 1):
                adj[("R", r_idx)].append(("ZR", z_idx))
    for z_idx, (zi, zj) in enumerate(Z):
        for c_idx, (i, s) in enumerate(C):
            if i == zi and ((out1[s] >> zj) & 1):
                adj[("ZL", z_idx)].append(("C", c_idx))
    for r_idx, c_idx in conflicts:
        adj[("R", r_idx)].append(("C", c_idx))
    match_L, match_R = {}, {}
    for u in V_L:
        visited = set()
        def dfs(x):
            for y in adj[x]:
                if y in visited:
                    continue
                visited.add(y)
                if y not in match_R or dfs(match_R[y]):
                    match_L[x] = y
                    match_R[y] = x
                    return True
            return False
        dfs(u)
    reached_L, reached_R = set(), set()
    q = deque([u for u in V_L if u not in match_L])
    for u in q:
        reached_L.add(u)
    while q:
        u = q.popleft()
        for v in adj[u]:
            if match_L.get(u) != v and v not in reached_R:
                reached_R.add(v)
                if v in match_R:
                    w = match_R[v]
                    if w not in reached_L:
                        reached_L.add(w)
                        q.append(w)
    cover_L = set(V_L) - reached_L
    cover_R = set(reached_R)
    R_keep = [list(R[k]) for k in range(len(R)) if ("R", k) not in cover_L]
    C_keep = [list(C[k]) for k in range(len(C)) if ("C", k) not in cover_R]
    uncovered_Z = 0
    for zi, zj in Z:
        cov_r = any(j == zj and ((out1[p] >> zi) & 1) for p, j in R_keep)
        cov_c = any(i == zi and ((out1[s] >> zj) & 1) for i, s in C_keep)
        if not (cov_r or cov_c):
            uncovered_Z += 1
    return {
        "R_prime": R_keep,
        "C_prime": C_keep,
        "Z_size": len(Z),
        "H_size": len(H),
        "uncovered_Z": uncovered_Z,
        "deleted": (len(R) - len(R_keep)) + (len(C) - len(C_keep))
    }

def main():
    inp = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
    n = int(inp["n"])
    if n < 1 or n > 7:
        sys.exit(2)
    out1 = [0] * n
    in1 = [0] * n
    seen_arcs = set()
    for u, v in inp["arcs"]:
        if not (0 <= u < n and 0 <= v < n) or u == v or (u, v) in seen_arcs or (v, u) in seen_arcs:
            sys.exit(2)
        seen_arcs.add((u, v))
        out1[u] |= (1 << v)
        in1[v] |= (1 << u)
    out2 = [0] * n
    seymour_vertices = []
    max_slack = None
    for v in range(n):
        f2 = 0
        for u in range(n):
            if (out1[v] >> u) & 1:
                f2 |= out1[u]
        out2[v] = f2 & ~((1 << v) | out1[v])
        d1 = out1[v].bit_count()
        d2 = out2[v].bit_count()
        if d1 <= d2:
            seymour_vertices.append(v)
        slack = d2 - d1
        if max_slack is None or slack > max_slack:
            max_slack = slack
    min_subset_deficit = 0
    if n >= 2:
        min_subset_deficit = None
        for s_mask in range(1, (1 << n) - 1):
            f1_s = 0
            for u in range(n):
                if (s_mask >> u) & 1:
                    f1_s |= out1[u]
            f2_s = 0
            for u in range(n):
                if (f1_s >> u) & 1:
                    f2_s |= out1[u]
            diff = (f2_s & ~f1_s).bit_count() - (f1_s & ~s_mask).bit_count()
            if min_subset_deficit is None or diff < min_subset_deficit:
                min_subset_deficit = diff
    eta_univ = solve_universal_eta(n, out1, out2)
    vw = [Q(str(x)) for x in inp.get("vertex_weights", [1] * n)]
    weighted_vertices = []
    for v in range(n):
        w1 = sum((vw[u] for u in range(n) if (out1[v] >> u) & 1), Q(0))
        w2 = sum((vw[u] for u in range(n) if (out2[v] >> u) & 1), Q(0))
        if w1 <= w2:
            weighted_vertices.append(v)
    w_map = {}
    for u, v, w_val in inp.get("arc_weights", []):
        w_map[(int(u), int(v))] = Q(str(w_val))
    arc_weighted_vertices = []
    for v in range(n):
        lhs = Q(0)
        for s in range(n):
            best = Q(0)
            for u in range(n):
                if (out1[v] >> u) & 1:
                    if (out1[u] >> s) & 1:
                        cand = w_map.get((u, s), Q(0)) - (w_map.get((v, s), Q(0)) if ((out1[v] >> s) & 1) else Q(0))
                        if cand > best:
                            best = cand
            lhs += best
        rhs = sum((w_map.get((v, u), Q(0)) for u in range(n) if (out1[v] >> u) & 1), Q(0))
        if lhs >= rhs:
            arc_weighted_vertices.append(v)
    m_blk = int(inp.get("blowup_m", 2))
    blowup_max_lhs = 0
    for mask in range(1, 1 << m_blk):
        f1_m = 0
        for a in range(m_blk):
            if (mask >> a) & 1:
                for b in range(a + 1, m_blk):
                    f1_m |= (1 << b)
        f2_m = 0
        for a in range(m_blk):
            if (f1_m >> a) & 1:
                for b in range(a + 1, m_blk):
                    f2_m |= (1 << b)
        val = mask.bit_count() - 2 * f1_m.bit_count() + f2_m.bit_count()
        if val > blowup_max_lhs:
            blowup_max_lhs = val
    pr_in = inp.get("pruning", {"R": [[i, i] for i in range(n)], "C": [[i, i] for i in range(n)]})
    pr_res = prune_families(out1, pr_in["R"], pr_in["C"])
    has_c3 = False
    for v in range(n):
        if out2[v] & in1[v]:
            has_c3 = True
            break
    full_mask = (1 << n) - 1
    non_neighbor_vertices = []
    for v in range(n):
        z_mask = full_mask & ~((1 << v) | out1[v] | in1[v])
        d_plus = out1[v].bit_count()
        if d_plus <= z_mask.bit_count():
            non_neighbor_vertices.append(v)
    out = {
        "seymour_vertices": seymour_vertices,
        "max_slack": max_slack,
        "min_subset_deficit": min_subset_deficit,
        "universal_eta": eta_univ,
        "weighted_vertices": weighted_vertices,
        "arc_weighted_vertices": arc_weighted_vertices,
        "blowup_max_lhs": blowup_max_lhs,
        "pruning": pr_res,
        "c3_free": not has_c3,
        "non_neighbor_vertices": non_neighbor_vertices
    }
    print(json.dumps(out, sort_keys=True))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

z_mask.bit_count():\n            non_neighbor_vertices.append(v)\n    out = {\n        \"seymour_vertices\": seymour_vertices,\n        \"max_slack\": max_slack,\n        \"min_subset_deficit\": blowup_max_lhs,\n        \"pruning\": pr_res,\n        \"c3_free\": not has_c3,\n        \"non_neighbor_vertices\": non_neighbor_vertices\n    }\n    print(json.dumps(out, sort_keys=True))\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n",
    "test_vectors": [

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper establishes a pure graph-theoretic neighborhood inequality on finite oriented graphs and makes no claims regarding software systems, execution behavior, reliability, or defect bounds. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper studies static finite oriented graphs, tournaments, and binary relations, with no game payoffs, strategic equilibria, or simulation dynamics. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No developer tools, static analyzers, compilers, or operational utilities are specified or evaluated in the source text. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The paper guarantees structural witnesses in finite oriented graphs, including a vertex $v$ with $\lvert N_1^+(v)\rvert\leq\lvert N_2^+(v)\rvert$, a directed $3$-cycle whenever $\delta^+(D)\geq n/3$ and $\delta^-(D)\geq n/3$, and a bipartite alternating-path pruning construction for finite binary relations that eliminates all conflicts between pair families $\mathcal R,\mathcal C\subseteq X\times X$ while retaining conflict-free members. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper operates on abstract finite vertex sets, arc sets, pair families, and real matrices without designing or analyzing concrete memory layouts or data structures. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardware architectures, circuit topologies, or accelerator execution substrates are discussed in the paper. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No cryptographic primitives, hardness assumptions, security protocols, or adversary models appear in the paper. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No statistical learning models, training objectives, or neural network architectures appear in the paper. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The paper establishes linear-programming dual weightings on oriented graphs via Farkas' lemma in Corollary 8, bounds maximum matchings and minimum vertex covers in the augmented conflict graph $\Gamma$ in Lemma 5, and optimizes the extremal objective $M+d$ over compatible pair families with $\Delta\subseteq P\cap Q$ in Proposition 7. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No distributed protocols, message-passing semantics, consensus algorithms, or fault-tolerance models are formulated in the paper. |
| other (combinatorics_and_graph_theory) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The paper resolves Seymour's second neighborhood conjecture for all nonempty finite oriented graphs (Theorem 1), establishes subset-deficit and transitive-tournament blowup reductions (Lemmas 3 and 4), rules out strict subset concavity of the one-step image operator (Proposition 7), and deduces vertex-weighted, arc-weighted, and directed-cycle consequences (Corollaries 8 and 9). |
| other (linear_algebra_and_matrix_theory) | True | moderate | adaptable | stated | Lemma 6 proves that for any real matrix $D:\mathbb R^J\to\mathbb R^I$ of maximum rank among matrices supported on $T\subseteq I\times J$, vectors $x\in\ker D$ and $y\in\ker D^{\mathsf T}$ satisfy $x_jy_s=0$ on all $(s,j)\in T$, which combined with the commuting matrix factorization $NL=AB$ bounds the maximum matching size in Lemma 5. |

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-subset-deficit",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a finite nonempty oriented graph such that $\\lvert N_1^+(v)\\rvert>\\lvert N_2^+(v)\\rvert$ for every $v\\in V(D)$. Choose $D$ with minimum order and, subject to that, minimum number of arcs among all graphs with this property.",
          "source_object": "lem:subset-deficit"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-blowup",
          "exact_quote": "For an integer $m>n$, replace each vertex by a transitive tournament on $m$ vertices. For every arc $v\\to w$ of $D$, put all arcs from the block at $v$ to the block at $w$, and put no other arcs between distinct blocks.",
          "source_object": "lem:blowup"
        },
        {

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): Theorem 1 and its corollaries require a nonempty finite oriented graph, defined as a finite directed graph with no loops, no multiple arcs, and no pair of opposite arcs. Blocked use: ['Applying the second-neighborhood inequality directly to infinite directed graphs or to the empty graph.', 'Applying the theorem to general directed graphs containing self-loops, parallel multiple arcs, or opposite arcs without first reducing to an oriented graph.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): The second out-neighborhood counts only distinct vertices at directed distance exactly two, excluding the source vertex and all first out-neighbors, with each reached vertex counted once regardless of the number of two-step paths. Blocked use: ['Bounding the number of directed walks of length two or counting two-step endpoints with path multiplicity.', 'Retaining vertices already reached in one step inside the second out-neighborhood count.']
- **L3** (edge_case): The underlying undirected graph need not be complete or connected, and any sink vertex trivially satisfies the conclusion of Theorem 1 with empty first and second out-neighborhoods. Blocked use: ['Assuming that a witness vertex satisfying the second-neighborhood inequality must have positive outdegree when the oriented graph contains sinks.', 'Inferring graph connectivity or tournament completeness from the existence of a valid second-neighborhood vertex.']
- **L4** (nonconstructive): The proof of Theorem 1 proceeds by contradiction from a hypothetical counterexample of minimum order and minimum arc count, amplified by a transitive-tournament blowup and ruled out by an extremal pair-pruning argument; it is existential and provides no algorithm faster than direct neighborhood inspection. Blocked use: ['Treating the counterexample-reduction, transitive-tournament blowup, or maximum-rank matrix perturbation steps as a constructive runtime algorithm for locating a witness vertex.', 'Claiming sub-exhaustive vertex-search complexity from the proof steps of Theorem 1.']
- **L5** (counterexample_scope): The strict subset-deficit inequality in Lemma 3 and the strict subset-growth inequality in Proposition 2 and Lemma 4 apply only to hypothetical counterexamples and their transitive-tournament blowups with all positive indegrees; Proposition 7 proves that no nonempty finite oriented graph with positive indegrees can satisfy the strict subset-growth inequality for all nonempty proper subsets. Blocked use: ['Applying the strict subset-deficit inequality of Lemma 3 or the strict subset-growth inequality of Proposition 2 as valid properties of actual nonempty finite oriented graphs.', 'Applying Lemma 4 without the hypotheses that all vertices of the base oriented graph have positive indegree and that the integer block size m strictly exceeds the base order n.']
- **L6** (formal_scope): Lemma 5 (Pruning) holds for every finite set with an arbitrary binary relation and does not require the relation to be an oriented graph, provided that the two pair families are counted as separate copies and that the deletion bound includes both the conflict-source set H and the points of Z left uncovered by the surviving subfamilies. Blocked use: ['Applying the pruning deletion bound of Lemma 5 while omitting the term counting points of Z left uncovered after pruning.', 'Assuming that the conflict-free subfamilies produced by Lemma 5 leave all conflict targets in Z covered.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): In Lemma 6 (Kernels at maximum support rank), the coordinate orthogonality relation between the kernel of D and the kernel of its transpose holds only when the real matrix D achieves the maximum possible rank among all real matrices supported on the finite index relation T. Blocked use: ['Applying the kernel coordinate orthogonality conclusion of Lemma 6 to an arbitrary matrix supported on T whose rank is strictly below the maximum support rank.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): Corollary 8 requires nonnegative vertex or arc weightings; in the universal vertex-weighting assertion of Corollary 8, the normalized weighting need not assign strictly positive weight to every vertex, and in the arc-weighted assertion, two-step paths are determined by the structural arcs of the oriented graph even when an arc has weight zero. Blocked use: ['Applying Corollary 8 to signed weightings that take negative values on vertices or arcs.', 'Assuming that the universal vertex weighting in Corollary 8 has strictly positive support on every vertex.', 'Treating an arc of weight zero in Corollary 8 as a missing arc when determining eligible two-step paths.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): In Corollary 9, the non-neighbor degree inequality requires the absence of directed 3-cycles, the directed triangle existence guarantee requires both minimum outdegree and minimum indegree to be at least n/3, and the order lower bound requires regularity of both in- and outdegree equal to r and directed girth exactly four. Blocked use: ['Applying the non-neighbor inequality of Corollary 9 to oriented graphs that contain directed 3-cycles.', 'Inferring the existence of a directed 3-cycle from Corollary 9 when only one of minimum outdegree or minimum indegree is at least n/3.', 'Applying the order lower bound 3r+1 of Corollary 9 to irregular oriented graphs or to graphs whose directed girth is not four.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Scope note of family 173 (`lean/docs/173.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A proof of Seymour’s second-neighborhood conjecture](../../preprints/A-proof-of-Seymours-second-neighborhood-conjecture-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Seymour's second-neighborhood conjecture asserts that every nonempty finite oriented graph has a vertex with at least as many second out-neighbors as first out-neighbors. The formalized result proves this assertion, where the second neighborhood consists of vertices at directed distance exactly two. The initial vertex and first neighbors are excluded from that count, and sinks are included.

Comparator source `SeymourSecondNeighborhood.lean` (913 bytes, sha256 `fa4a37831405b3fe7f6a88a2dce8918982195ee1757e05e4623055822d36ca98`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib.Data.Fintype.Card
import Mathlib.Data.Finset.Union

namespace OAI

namespace SeymourSecondNeighborhood

variable {V : Type*}

structure IsOriented (r : V → V → Prop) : Prop where
  loopless : ∀ v, ¬ r v v
  asymmetric : ∀ {u v}, r u v → ¬ r v u

variable [Fintype V] [DecidableEq V]

noncomputable section

def firstNeighbors (r : V → V → Prop) (v : V) : Finset V := by
  classical
  exact Finset.univ.filter (r v)

def secondNeighbors (r : V → V → Prop) (v : V) : Finset V := by
  classical
  exact Finset.univ.filter (fun w =>
    w ≠ v ∧ ¬ r v w ∧ ∃ u, r v u ∧ r u w)

def GoodVertex (r : V → V → Prop) (v : V) : Prop :=
  (firstNeighbors r v).card ≤ (secondNeighbors r v).card

end

variable [Nonempty V]

theorem exists_goodVertex (r : V → V → Prop) (hr : IsOriented r) :
    ∃ v, GoodVertex r v := by
  sorry

end SeymourSecondNeighborhood

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every nonempty finite oriented graph has a vertex with at least as many vertices at directed distance two as at directed distance one. This resolves Seymour's second neighborhood conjecture positively.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| [stmt-prop-counterexample-reduction](#stmt-prop-counterexample-reduction) | proposition | `prop:counterexample-reduction` | 2 | If Theorem 1 has a counterexample, then there is a finite nonempty oriented graph $G$ in which every vertex has positive indegree and |
| [stmt-lem-subset-deficit](#stmt-lem-subset-deficit) | lemma | `lem:subset-deficit` | 3 | Let $D$ be a finite nonempty oriented graph such that $\lvert N_1^+(v)\rvert>\lvert N_2^+(v)\rvert$ for every $v\in V(D)$. |
| [stmt-lem-blowup](#stmt-lem-blowup) | lemma | `lem:blowup` | 4 | Let $D$ be an oriented graph on $n\ge1$ vertices, all of positive indegree, satisfying [eq:subset-deficit] for every nonempty proper subset of $V(D)$. |
